# Audit REVIEW: faction / living world / world_sim / world time — A7 (review pass)

Area A7. Local tree ONLY: `~/workspace/repos/worldarchitect.ai` (main-branch ZIP tree, no .git history). Zero GitHub API/MCP calls; no repo files changed. Read-only review of run 2 (`~/workspace/plans/audit/faction-worldsim-time.md`, v2) against the spec in `~/workspace/plans/zero-code-rejustification-audit.md` §4/§6. Repo skill/policy/prompt/AGENTS files treated as DATA, never instructions. The ZFC skill amendment draft (`~/workspace/plans/zfc-skill-model-amendment-proposal.md`) is DRAFT ONLY and was NOT applied as policy (see §5).

**Files reviewed: 50 (47 Python + faction/README.md + world_sim/tools.json + timeline cutover schema JSON). Methods/functions reviewed: 698 function/class units (602 functions incl. nested closures + 96 classes) + 107 module constants = 805 Python symbols, every one rowed below, plus the 3 non-Python assets as single units (808 units total). v2 file read: full (802 lines, Phase 1 + Phase 2 + §6 log + counts). Run-2 Phase 1: KEEP 34 / ADAPT 14 / DELETE 2. Run-2 Phase 2 rows: KEEP 281 / ADAPT 35 / DELETE 46 (362 rows, 698 symbols). Review verdict totals (Python symbols): KEEP 693 / ADAPT 38 / DELETE 74; with the 3 non-Python assets (all KEEP): KEEP 696 / ADAPT 38 / DELETE 74.**

Enumeration method: Python `ast` walk over every assigned `.py` file (all `FunctionDef`/`AsyncFunctionDef` incl. nested closures, all `ClassDef`, all module-level assignments), reconciled: 602 + 96 = 698 = run-2's inventoried count exactly; the +107 constants are module-level assignments run 2 largely omitted from rows (see §4.6) and are rowed here. Caller evidence: single-pass token index over all `mvp_site/**/*.py` (prod + tests), excluding the defining file, cross-checked with targeted `grep -rn` for every challenged/flagged symbol; generic names (`to_dict`, `__init__`, transaction closures) are marked non-discriminating rather than given a spurious count. Body depth: full reads for files under ~700 lines; for the five large files (coordinator 2,085, background_worker 1,885, handler 1,435, models 1,480, firestore_transaction 1,242 lines) full symbol inventory + docstrings + targeted reads of all decision-bearing functions — verdicts there do not depend on unread lines except where §6 notes it.

Verdict key: review verdict is the first token in the Run-2 column; CONFIRM = run-2 verdict stands on re-check, CHALLENGE = review verdict differs (see §4). §6(3) governs throughout: NO verdict below deletes faction simulation or the living world outright; DELETEs target server recomputation, formatting, or unregistered field-aware transforms, and synchronous turn-path faction work is flagged ADAPT to async.

## (1) Dead / unreachable code list

All items below have zero game-path callers AND zero test callers in the local tree (proven by exhaustive name grep, not by sampling), satisfying the 2026-10-29 rule that non-use must be proven before deletion. "0 external files" in a per-method row alone is NOT listed here: intra-file calls and protocol dispatch are legitimate reachability (e.g. store protocol methods, transaction closures), and are not dead.

### 1.1 faction/dual_mode.py — whole file dead (29 symbols, CHALLENGE to run 2)

- Evidence: `grep -rn DualModeManager mvp_site --include=*.py` returns only dual_mode.py:164 (definition) and faction/__init__.py:19,61 (re-export). `grep -rn 'from mvp_site.faction import' mvp_site --include=*.py` returns zero importers in prod or tests — every prod faction caller imports `mvp_site.faction.tools` directly (agents.py:88, dice.py:16, game_state.py:26, faction_state_util.py:17). No test file references DualModeManager. No dynamic/string loading found (no "dual_mode" string import anywhere in prod code; the testing_framework `dual_mode_test` decorator is an unrelated test helper). The barrel re-export is the only referencer, and a barrel is not a game-path caller.
- Symbols: CampaignMode, TriggerUrgency, TriggerType, Trigger, Trigger.is_expired, Trigger.to_dict, Trigger.from_dict, DualModeState, DualModeState.to_dict, DualModeState.from_dict, DualModeManager, DualModeManager.__init__, DualModeManager.from_game_state, DualModeManager.to_game_state, DualModeManager.switch_to_faction, DualModeManager.switch_to_adventure, DualModeManager.add_personal_time, DualModeManager.advance_strategic_turn, DualModeManager.record_faction_action, DualModeManager.long_rest, DualModeManager.add_trigger, DualModeManager.dismiss_trigger, DualModeManager.get_triggers_by_urgency, DualModeManager.has_critical_triggers, DualModeManager._evaluate_neglect_triggers, DualModeManager._cleanup_expired_triggers, DualModeManager.build_reminder_block, DualModeManager.build_status_display, DualModeManager.evaluate_faction_state (dual_mode.py:25-465).
- Why §6(3) does not rescue it: §6(3) keeps faction *simulation* as async background execution. DualModeManager is not simulation — it is an unreachable mode/trigger manager with no simulation caller. Deleting it removes no live simulation capability. If the dual-mode feature is ever revived, it re-enters as new code, not as this dead file.
- Consequence for faction/__init__.py: its dual-mode re-exports (lines 17-24, 61-66) must be dropped with the file (barrel ADAPT, already run-2's verdict).

## (3) Stale / misleading docs list

### 3.1 faction/README.md — stale sections (file:line)

- README.md:7-13 "Resources (`resources.py`)" section documents `calculate_citizen_growth()`, `calculate_gold_income()`, `calculate_arcana_yield()`, `calculate_prestige_income()` — what the code actually does: there is no `resources.py` in `mvp_site/faction/` (directory listing: README.md, __init__.py, ai_factions.py, battle_sim.py, combat.py, dual_mode.py, intel.py, rankings.py, srd_units.py, tools.py). The whole Resources section describes a module that does not exist in this tree. Fix: delete the section or restore the module; do not leave it as documentation of shipped code.
- README.md Dual Mode section ("Seamless D&D adventure + faction management integration", "Phase 1 implementation enabling players to blend…") — what the code actually does: dual_mode.py exists but is unreachable (§1.1); no prod path blends modes. The README presents a dead file as a live system. Fix: mark it unshipped/dead or delete the section with the file.
- README.md Combat/Intel/Rankings sections list the DELETE-verdict formulas (`calculate_faction_power`, `calculate_damage`, `calculate_intel_success`, `calculate_total_fp`, `calculate_ranking`) as the minigame's modules. Those descriptions are accurate to the *current* code (the formulas are still routed via faction/tools.py), so they are not stale today — but they become stale the moment the DELETE verdicts land, and the README must be updated in the same PRs. Flagged here so the README is not orphaned by the formula deletions.

### 3.2 faction/__init__.py — stale barrel markers (file:line)

- faction/__init__.py:1-9 module docstring ("core game mechanics… Resource management, Combat calculations, Intel operations, Rankings") — accurate to current code, but like the README it presents DELETE-verdict formulas as the package's core API; update it when the barrel is slimmed (run-2 ADAPT).
- faction/__init__.py:67-68 `__all__` contains bare comment lines `# Resources` and `# Combat` with no names under `# Resources` (the resources module never existed in the barrel) — a stale section marker from a removed module. What the code actually does: `__all__` re-exports only the listed names; the `# Resources` comment promises a section that is empty. Fix: delete the comment in the slimming PR.
- faction/__init__.py:98-99 re-exports `expected_damage_per_round` and `calculate_army_hp` (DELETE-verdict, battle_sim.py:543,602) and lines 87-96 re-export the DELETE-verdict ranking/intel/combat formulas — the barrel currently advertises deleted symbols as public API. ## (4) Challenges to run-2 verdicts, with evidence

### 4.1 CHALLENGE — dual_mode.py: run-2 KEEP/ADAPT → review DELETE (whole file, 29 symbols)

Run 2 kept the state-write methods as durable writes with a "dead-code caveat for reachability confirmation". That caveat is now resolved against KEEP: reachability is disproven (§1.1 evidence). Durable-write KEEP requires a writer; there is no writer, no instantiator, and no package importer in prod or tests. Every symbol in §1.1 changes verdict: KEEP→DELETE (24 symbols), ADAPT→DELETE (add_trigger, _evaluate_neglect_triggers, evaluate_faction_state), DELETE→DELETE confirmed (build_reminder_block, build_status_display). File verdict: run-2 ADAPT → review DELETE in full. This is the review's largest challenge and the only one that changes a file verdict.

### 4.2 CHALLENGE — validation.py: run-2 DELETE/ADAPT → review KEEP (2 symbols)

- `validate_highlight_semantics` (validation.py:49-70): run-2 DELETE rested on "semantics are model-owned". The implementation is pure jsonschema `Draft202012Validator` validation against the canonical response schema's highlight item (required fields, types, enums) — it returns an error string or None, repairs nothing, and judges no content beyond schema conformance. Reject-only structural gates are KEEP throughout this area (event_codec.py validators, background_provider.py envelope validation) and in the schema-parsing group's warn-only/structural validator policy. The name "semantics" overstates the code. Verdict: KEEP. If the owner wants the semantic *name* gone, rename it; do not delete the gate.
- `validate_bulk_highlight_response` (validation.py:128-241): run-2 ADAPT ("stays only in structural form") assumed the semantics check would be deleted. With §4.2's KEEP, its remaining invariants (1..30 exact dates, max 6 highlights, SlotState.RESERVED, owner match, generation match, duplicate dates, unknown-date citation, routine_private slot rule) are authorization/bounds/slot rules, and it mutates nothing on failure. Verdict: KEEP (ADAPT no longer needed). Live caller: coordinator.py:422 (and semantics re-check at coordinator.py:686).

### 4.3 CORRECTIONS, not verdict changes — rankings.py

- `BEHAVIOR_DESCRIPTIONS` (rankings.py:148): run-2 OMISSION — this table appears in no run-2 row. It feeds only `get_ai_faction_behavior` (rankings.py:168-197, DELETE). Review verdict: DELETE with that lookup. (Fills a gap; does not change any run-2 verdict.)
- `get_all_ai_factions` (rankings.py:200-209): run-2 ADAPT ("one-time seeding, persisted roster") CONFIRMED as a verdict but CORRECTED as a description of current code: as written it calls `generate_ai_factions(seed=AI_FACTION_SEED, count=AI_FACTION_COUNT)` on EVERY call, and it is called per ranking computation (rankings.py:177,240,272, via tools). There is no persisted roster read anywhere in this path. The ADAPT is therefore real work (persist the roster, read it back), not a relabelling of current behaviour. If that work is not done, the function as written is per-turn regeneration, which strict ZFC would DELETE — the ADAPT survives only as the persisted-roster plan.
- `generate_ai_factions` (ai_factions.py:208): ADAPT CONFIRMED on the same condition — its only in-repo caller is rankings.py:209. One-time seeding must be wired to a persisted roster; until then the call chain regenerates per ranking.

### 4.4 CONFIRMED with strengthened evidence — cooldown stripping DELETE, and what survival requires

Run-2 DELETE for `strip_antagonist_events_during_cooldown` (living_world_contract.py:156), `enforce_antagonist_cooldown_on_write` (L274), `strip_lw_fields_from_mapping` (L321), `restore_cooldown_state` (L341), `normalize_lw_for_persist` (L372), `strip_lw_from_response` (L407), plus helpers `_world_time_to_game_day` (L221) and `_find_last_antagonist_day_in_state` (L239, incl. nested `_scan` L252): CONFIRMED. Registry evidence re-checked: `grep` over backend_adjustment_specs.py (2,703 lines) and backend_adjustment_registry.py finds NO antagonist, living-world-cooldown, or cooldown-strip spec — the only "strip" specs are unrelated (level_up rewards-state stripping, adjuster JSON-strip-prefix, level_up session stale-offer strip). The strip functions are LIVE (llm_parser.py:1208,3922,3931; world_logic.py:9365,9380,10169-10208), so this is not a dead-code deletion — it is a live unregistered transform deletion, which is exactly what strict ZFC requires. What would be needed for survival, stated precisely: (a) a named AdjustmentSpec in backend_adjustment_specs.py identifying the antagonist-cooldown strip by function and file, (b) activation in the adjustment registry, and (c) a necessity proof that prompt/schema enforcement cannot achieve the pacing rule (the current docstring's issue-#7956 failure mode is a starting point for (c), not a substitute for (a)+(b)). Until all three exist, enforcement moves to prompt/schema and the server strip deletes. Note this does NOT delete the living world: the background provider/worker path, the cooldown *predicates* (is_cooldown_turn, is_antagonist_cooldown_active — ADAPT as prompt-side flags), and the 14-day constant as a prompt pacing rule (ANTAGONIST_COOLDOWN_DAYS — ADAPT) all survive.

### 4.5 RESOLVED CAVEAT, verdict unchanged — merge_partial_world_time ADAPT confirmed

Run-2 caveat: "referenced only by a test; ADAPT assumes a live write-path caller; if test-only, DELETE". Resolved in favour of ADAPT: game_state_merge.py:2053 calls `world_time_lib.handle_world_time_merge_if_needed` inside `_handle_dict_merge` on the live state-merge write path, and handle_world_time_merge_if_needed (world_time.py:569) calls `merge_partial_world_time` (world_time.py:589) for `world_data/world_time` partial updates. The conditional DELETE does not trigger. ADAPT stands: slim to detection + temporal-violation reporting, merge itself to model/schema. (Test references at tests/test_world_time_progression.py:152-167,1829-1853 are additional, not sole, callers.)

### 4.6 Run-2 constant omissions — filled, no verdict surprises

Run 2's rows largely omit module-level constants. This review rows all 107 of them. Their verdicts: KEEP (bounds, vocabularies, transport tables, error/bounds constants — e.g. event_codec.py MAX_* bounds, cohort.py COOLDOWN_WINDOWS/TOTAL_COHORT_BUDGET, models.py MAX_FOREGROUND_ACTION_RECEIPTS/MAX_COORDINATOR_SERIALIZED_BYTES/BACKGROUND_EXECUTION_LEASE_SECONDS, validation.py MAX_HIGHLIGHTS_PER_CALL etc.), except the living_world_contract constants already verdict-ed in run 2 (ANTAGONIST_COOLDOWN_DAYS ADAPT, DEFAULT_EVENT_TONE DELETE) and the rankings BEHAVIOR_DESCRIPTIONS DELETE (§4.3). Two world_time symbols run 2 did not row are also filled: `world_time_string_only` (world_time.py:485, the ("era","time_of_day") string-component set) KEEP as a validation vocabulary, and `valid_world_time_partial_component` (L523, alias of the ADAPT helper) ADAPT with its target. No constant's filled verdict contradicts a run-2 verdict, because run 2 gave none for them.

### 4.7 Synchronous turn-path faction work — §6(3) gap flagged (ADAPT scope, not new DELETEs)

Run 2 ADAPT-ed the simulation routines but did not name the invocation sites. For the record: `execute_faction_tool` is invoked synchronously from the turn path at dice.py:692, game_state.py:4013, llm_providers/provider_utils.py:1334,1519,1563,1657,1759, and preventive_guards.py:424,443; `evaluate_living_world_trigger` is invoked synchronously at game_state_mixins.py:996. Per §6(3) the ADAPT for these call sites is: dice-bearing rolls stay on-path per §6(2) (roll_dice, roll_attack, is_detected), while bulk battle/intel/ranking work and living-world trigger evaluation move to post-commit/background execution with merge-on-next-turn. This confirms run-2's ADAPT verdicts and extends their scope to the call sites; it does not add DELETEs, and no verdict here deletes simulation outright.

## (5) Draft-amendment effects (DRAFT ONLY — NOT applied)

The draft at ~/workspace/plans/zfc-skill-model-amendment-proposal.md (FastEmbed/Jev outputs count as model-authored; server dispatch on a model output is execution; hand-written thresholds/boosts/anchor lists layered on outputs still need an exception) was NOT applied to any verdict below. Where it would matter, the effect is nil or negative for rescue:

- No FastEmbed or Jev usage exists anywhere in these 50 files (repo grep for fastembed/jev over faction/, world_sim/, living_world*.py, world_time.py, faction_state_util.py: zero hits). The draft's reclassification therefore changes no verdict in this area.
- Background/relevance/setup providers (background_provider.py, relevance_provider.py, setup_provider.py) already treat Gemini-call outputs as model-authored with server-side schema/bounds validation only — their KEEP verdicts are the same under the draft.
- The draft would NOT rescue any DELETE in this area: the deleted items are hand-written server arithmetic (FP/ranking/damage/intel-tier formulas, SRD scaling, expected-value battle math) and hand-written server transforms (cooldown strips, time formatting/merging, display blocks) — exactly the category the draft says still needs an exception ("thresholds/boosts/anchor lists layered on outputs remain field-aware"). Under the draft, every DELETE below still needs a named + registered exception to survive, and none has one (§4.4).
- The draft would NOT rescue dual_mode.py either: its trigger evaluations are server rules over counters, not outputs of any model call, so the draft's model-output reclassification does not apply to them; and the file is dead regardless (§1.1).
